    }

    /** Legacy no-op kept so old call sites keep compiling. */
    fun setSmartMode(enabled: Boolean) {
        if (enabled) selectSmartMode()
    }

    private val coreBinaryMissingLogged = java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicBoolean(false)

    fun pingAll() {
        pingNodeListInternal(nodeEntities.value)
    }

    /**
     * Auto-initiated bulk pass, capped at [AUTO_PING_CAP] nodes. The manual
     * Ping All button goes through [pingAll] directly and is never capped —
     * the user asked for a full run and the per-row spinner is expected.
     * This wrapper only narrows the cold-start path so a subscription with
     * hundreds of entries stays responsive.
     */
    private fun pingAllCapped() {
        val all = nodeEntities.value
        if (all.size <= AUTO_PING_CAP) {
            pingNodeListInternal(all)
        } else {
            log("Auto-ping cap: measuring ${AUTO_PING_CAP} of ${all.size} node(s); tap Ping All for the rest")
            pingNodeListInternal(all.take(AUTO_PING_CAP))
        }
    }

    /**
     * One-shot "ping on open" pass. The flag lives here instead of a Compose
     * `remember` so navigating away from the Servers tab and back does not
     * restart the entire list check. Called from ServerListScreen's LaunchedEffect.
     */
    fun onServerListOpenedOnce() {
        if (!_settings.value.pingAutoOnOpen) return
        if (!autoPingOnOpenDone.compareAndSet(false, true)) return
        if (_isPinging.value) return
        if (nodeEntities.value.isEmpty()) return
        pingAll()
    }

    fun pingGroup(subscriptionId: String?) {
        val targets = nodeEntities.value.filter { it.subscriptionId == subscriptionId }
        pingNodeListInternal(targets)
    }

    fun pingNodes(nodes: List<NodeUiModel>) {
        val nodeIds = nodes.map { it.id }.toSet()
        val targets = nodeEntities.value.filter { it.id in nodeIds }
        pingNodeListInternal(targets)
    }

    fun stopPing() {
        val wasRunning = bulkPingJob?.isActive == true || _isPinging.value
        bulkPingGeneration += 1
        bulkPingJob?.cancel()
        bulkPingJob = null
        _pingingNodeIds.value = emptySet()
        _testingNodeId.value = null
        _isPinging.value = false
        _smartTesting.value = false
        if (wasRunning) log("Ping stopped")
    }

    /** Timestamp + id-set of the last auto-ping that finished, so
     *  onAppForeground can skip a repeat pass on the same set. Manual
     *  pings (Ping All / single-node Check) update this too, because a
     *  fresh number is a fresh number regardless of what triggered it. */
    @Volatile private var lastAutoPingCompletedAt: Long = 0L
    @Volatile private var lastAutoPingIds: Set<String> = emptySet()
    /** True for the first onAppForeground of this process. Reset by the OS
     *  killing the process, so it lets us tell a cold start (clear stale
     *  pings, measure fresh) from a warm foreground (keep existing values,
     *  no re-trigger). */
    @Volatile private var processColdStart: Boolean = true

    private fun pingNodeListInternal(targets: List<NodeEntity>) {
        if (targets.isEmpty()) return
        val generation = ++bulkPingGeneration
        bulkPingJob?.cancel()
        val job = viewModelScope.launch(
            Dispatchers.IO,
            start = kotlinx.coroutines.CoroutineStart.LAZY
        ) {
            _isPinging.value = true
            _smartTesting.value = true
            try {
                val targetIds = targets.map { it.id }
                _serverTestResults.value = _serverTestResults.value - targetIds.toSet()
                _pingingNodeIds.value = targetIds.toSet()
                // Previous ping values are intentionally left in Room
                // while the new pass runs. The old reset-to-null made
                // every cold start show "—" on every row for the 6-8 s
                // the pass took, even though the previous numbers were
                // already in the database and still a reasonable
                // estimate. The spinner on each row (fed by
                // _pingingNodeIds) marks the row as under measurement;
                // the number updates the moment a fresh sample lands.
                // A ping pass that fails outright still ends with the
                // deadline path marking its leftovers as 0, so no stale
                // number outlives a genuinely unreachable node.

                // Real tunnel probe for every protocol, in the fastest
                // shape each one allows. urltest runs first: one sing-box
                // holds all the TCP-outbound nodes (VLESS/VMess/Trojan/SS)
                // and answers their Clash API /delay in parallel, so a
                // dozen rows land in a few seconds. The UDP-only nodes
                // (OVPN/WG/Hysteria2) cannot be expressed as raw outbounds
                // and are measured on the per-node path, where the
                // normalizer turns each one into a valid endpoint and the
                // full tunnel probe decides whether the handshake actually
                // completes. The user's ping-type setting no longer
                // decides the shape — the protocol does, because only a
                // tunnel round trip answers the question the row is meant
                // to answer: does this config actually work.
                val viaUrlTest = runCatching {
                    pingNodeListViaUrlTest(targets)
                }.getOrElse { e ->
                    log("urltest bulk ping crashed: ${e.javaClass.simpleName}: ${e.message}")
                    false
                }
                if (!viaUrlTest) {
                    log("urltest produced no result; running per-node fallback")
                    pingNodeListPerNode(targets, generation)
                }
                log("Ping finished for ${targets.size} node(s)")
                runCatching {
                    // TEMP DEBUG: see NodeDao.debugFindByNamePattern. Compare
                    // this list's size and ids against what the UI shows and
                    // against the "xray measured X/Y" ids logged above -
                    // that comparison alone tells us duplicate-DB-rows vs
                    // UI-cache with no ambiguity left.
                    val dbRows = nodeDao.debugFindByNamePattern("%xhttp%")
                    log(
                        "DEBUG xhttp-name rows in DB: ${dbRows.size} -> " +
                            dbRows.joinToString(" | ") { "${it.id.take(8)}:ping=${it.pingMs}" }
                    )
                }.onFailure { log("DEBUG dump failed: ${it.javaClass.simpleName}: ${it.message}") }
        lastAutoPingCompletedAt = System.currentTimeMillis()
        lastAutoPingIds = targets.map { it.id }.toSet()
            } finally {
                if (bulkPingGeneration == generation) {
                    bulkPingJob = null
                    _pingingNodeIds.value = emptySet()
                    _isPinging.value = false
                    _smartTesting.value = false
                }
            }
        }
        bulkPingJob = job
        job.start()
    }

    /**
     * Bulk ping through a single sing-box process. The config holds every
     * target as a named outbound; the Clash API answers one /delay per node
     * in parallel. Returns true when at least one node answered, so the
     * caller knows whether the per-node fallback is needed. Writes each
     * measured latency to Room and runs the auto-delete pass the old
     * per-node loop used to.
     */
    /**
     * Protocols sing-box accepts as a raw `outbounds[]` entry without the
     * endpoint/normalizer pass. Anything else (OpenVPN, WireGuard and its
     * variants, AmneziaWG, WARP) goes through the per-node path, which runs
     * the full buildConfig that knows how to convert those to a
     * sing-box-compatible endpoint.
     */
    private val URL_TEST_SCHEMES = setOf(
        // TCP-only schemes only. Hysteria/Hysteria2/TUIC sit behind a QUIC
        // handshake that needs the per-node readiness warmup this bulk path
        // does not run, so they are excluded here and left to the per-node
        // fallback via UDP_ONLY_SCHEMES. WireGuard/AmneziaWG/OpenVPN are
        // also UDP and already handled by that fallback.
        "vless", "vmess", "trojan",
        "shadowsocks", "ss", "ssr",
        "anytls", "mieru", "mierus",
        "socks", "socks5", "http", "https", "snell", "juicity"
    )

    private suspend fun kotlinx.coroutines.CoroutineScope.pingNodeListViaUrlTest(
        targets: List<NodeEntity>
    ): Boolean {
        val parsedList = targets.mapNotNull { entity ->
            runCatching { parseEntity(entity) }.getOrNull()?.let { entity.id to it }
        }
        if (parsedList.isEmpty()) {
            log("urltest: no parseable nodes in the batch")
            return false
        }

        // Split by protocol: an OpenVPN or WireGuard entry that sing-box
        // cannot decode aborts the whole batch with
        // "unknown outbound type: openvpn-client". Measured per scheme so a
        // single unsupported node never disables urltest for the rest.
        // Split first on the routing decision: xhttp and reality must be
        // measured by Xray, the rest keep their existing split. The
        // partition is by transport shape, not by short scheme, so it has
        // to happen before the scheme-based urltest/per-node split below.
        val (xrayNodes, nonXray) = parsedList.partition { pair ->
            com.lumen.core.config.builder.XrayRouter.shouldUseXray(pair.second)
        }
        val (urlTestable, perNodeOnly) = nonXray.partition { pair ->
            pair.second.scheme.trim().lowercase(java.util.Locale.US) in URL_TEST_SCHEMES
        }

        var anyResult = false
        var missedDeferred: kotlinx.coroutines.Deferred<Unit>? = null
        var xrayDeferred: kotlinx.coroutines.Deferred<Unit>? = null

        // Hoisted out of the urltest block below so the xray batch can
        // reach the application context too.
        val app = getApplication<Application>()

        // Xray batch. Xray has no Clash-style controller to fan a list
        // through one process, so each node is measured by its own Xray
        // spawn - the shape V2rayNG uses, and the only one whose number
        // matches what Connect will do afterwards. Launched first so it
        // overlaps the urltest and ovpn batches.
        val xrayParsed = xrayNodes.map { it.second }
        val xrayIds = xrayNodes.map { it.first }
        if (xrayParsed.isNotEmpty()) {
            log("urltest: ${xrayParsed.size} xray node(s): " +
                xrayParsed.joinToString(",") { it.name })
            anyResult = true
            xrayDeferred = async {
                // Bulk VLESS/Reality/SS/VMess/Trojan go through the process
                // based BulkSocksPinger for now: the in-process JNI probe
                // returns -1 on every node in the current build, and while
                // that is being diagnosed the bulk pass has to keep
                // producing numbers. Single-node checks still hit the JNI
                // path (performRealPing) and log XRAY_FAIL trace=, which is
                // the surface that tells us why the JNI measure fails.
                // In-process JNI probe (XrayPing). The previous process-based
                // BulkSocksPinger paid a runtime start + SOCKS loopback hop
                // per node and reported 3-5x the number V2rayNG shows for
                // the same server. Single-node checks already use XrayPing
                // via performRealPing.
                val result = com.lumen.app.ping.XrayPing(app).measure(
                    nodes = xrayParsed,
                    timeoutMs = 2_500,
                )
                log("xray bulk: ${result.log}")
                log("DEBUG xray input size=${xrayParsed.size} ids=${xrayIds.size} " +
                    "latencyKeys=${result.latencies.keys.toList().sorted()}")
                if (result.latencies.isNotEmpty()) {
                    val completedPings = mutableMapOf<String, Int>()
                    result.latencies.forEach { (index, ping) ->
                        val id = if (index in xrayIds.indices) xrayIds[index] else "OUT_OF_RANGE_$index"
                        log("DEBUG xray write index=$index id=${id.take(8)} ping=$ping")
                        nodeDao.updatePing(id, ping)
                        completedPings[id] = ping
                    }
                    if (_settings.value.pingAutoDeleteUnreachable) {
                        val removed = removeUnhealthyNodesAfterPing(
                            completedPings = completedPings,
                            thresholdMs = _settings.value.pingAutoDeleteThresholdMs
                        )
                        if (removed > 0) log("Auto-removed $removed xray node(s) after ping")
                    }
                }
                // Failed xray nodes whose transport sing-box can dial
                // (tcp / ws / grpc) get a second pass on the per-node
                // sing-box path. Only xhttp stream-one and reality keep
                // the "no retry" rule from the older router, since those
                // are the shapes sing-box mishandles.
                val missedXray = xrayParsed.filterIndexed { i, _ ->
                    i !in result.latencies.keys
                }
                val retryable = missedXray.filter { node ->
                    val stream = node.outbound["streamSettings"] as? Map<*, *>
                    val network = stream?.get("network")?.toString()?.lowercase() ?: "tcp"
                    val security = stream?.get("security")?.toString()?.lowercase() ?: ""
                    security != "reality" && network != "xhttp"
                }
                if (retryable.isNotEmpty()) {
                    val retryIds = retryable.mapNotNull { node ->
                        xrayIds.getOrNull(xrayParsed.indexOf(node))
                    }
                    val retryEntities = targets.filter { it.id in retryIds }
                    if (retryEntities.isNotEmpty()) {
                        // Route the retry through BulkSocksPinger(SINGBOX)
                        // directly. pingNodeListPerNode -> performRealPing
                        // consults XrayRouter again, which sends TLS/WS
                        // nodes right back to the same JNI core that just
                        // failed. The log then reads "retried on sing-box"
                        // while XRAY_CALLED fires a second time on the
                        // same dead path.
                        log("xray fallback: ${retryEntities.size} node(s) retried on sing-box bulk")
                        val retryParsed = retryEntities.mapNotNull { entity ->
                            runCatching { parseEntity(entity) }.getOrNull()
                        }
                        if (retryParsed.isNotEmpty()) {
                            val fallbackWorkDir = java.io.File(app.cacheDir, "xray-fallback")
                                .apply { mkdirs() }
                            val fallbackHostnames = (
                                retryParsed.map { it.server }.filter { it.isNotBlank() } +
                                    listOf("cp.cloudflare.com")
                                ).distinct()
                            val fallbackPinned = runCatching {
                                ServerAddressPinner.resolveForPing(fallbackHostnames)
                            }.getOrDefault(emptyMap())
                            val fallbackOptions = pingSingboxOptions(
                                socksPort = 0,
                                obfsPort = SingboxConfigBuilder.OBFS_LOCAL_PORT,
                                workDir = fallbackWorkDir,
                                pinnedServerIps = fallbackPinned
                            )
                            val fallbackResult = com.lumen.app.ping.BulkSocksPinger(app).measure(
                                nodes = retryParsed,
                                core = com.lumen.app.ping.BulkSocksPinger.Core.SINGBOX,
                                options = fallbackOptions,
                                timeoutMs = 2_500
                            )
                            log("xray fallback bulk: ${fallbackResult.log}")
                            if (fallbackResult.latencies.isNotEmpty()) {
                                val fallbackCompleted = mutableMapOf<String, Int>()
                                fallbackResult.latencies.forEach { entry ->
                                    val index = entry.key
                                    val ping = entry.value
                                    val id = retryEntities.getOrNull(index)?.id
                                    if (id != null) fallbackCompleted[id] = ping
                                }
                                if (fallbackCompleted.isNotEmpty()) {
                                    nodeDao.updatePingsBatch(
                                        fallbackCompleted.map { (id, ping) ->
                                            Pair(id, ping as Int?)
                                        }
                                    )
                                }
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }

        // Launch the OVPN/WG batch FIRST so it runs in parallel with
        // urltest. Both batches touch independent subsets of the list;
        // the previous order waited for urltest to finish and only then
        // started the per-node work, so a mixed subscription paid the
        // urltest wall clock plus the per-node wall clock back to back.
        val ovpnBatch = perNodeOnly.mapNotNull { pair ->
            targets.firstOrNull { it.id == pair.first }
        }
        val ovpnDeferred: kotlinx.coroutines.Deferred<Unit>? = if (ovpnBatch.isNotEmpty()) {
            log("urltest: ${ovpnBatch.size} non-urltest node(s) via per-node path")
            anyResult = true
            async { pingNodeListPerNode(ovpnBatch, bulkPingGeneration) }
        } else null

        if (urlTestable.isNotEmpty()) {
            val workDir = java.io.File(app.cacheDir, "urltest-options").apply { mkdirs() }
            // Pre-resolve every server hostname through a foreign resolver
            // and pin the answer. On an Iranian carrier the local resolver
            // answers foreign hostnames with the block-page IP, and without
            // this pin the bulk pass dials that address and reports every
            // node as unreachable. The per-node path already does exactly
            // this through ServerAddressPinner; the bulk path was missing
            // it.
            // Bound the outbound count one sing-box instance has to
            // hold. A 12-node list fits in one process; a subscription
            // with several hundred entries does not — startup time grows
            // with the config, memory grows with it, and an over-large
            // outbound list can abort the process before the Clash API
            // ever binds. 150 outbounds keeps startup under a second.
            val urlTestBatches = urlTestable.chunked(150)
            val allMissed = mutableListOf<NodeEntity>()

            for ((batchIndex, batch) in urlTestBatches.withIndex()) {
                val hostnames = (
                    batch.map { it.second.server }
                        .filter { it.isNotBlank() }
                    // Pin the ping target hostnames too: without them every
                    // /delay triggers a fresh DNS lookup through the
                    // configured resolver (1.1.1.1 UDP), which Iranian
                    // carriers throttle to 300-2000 ms. The measured
                    // latency then reports DNS + RTT instead of RTT.
                    + listOf(
                        "cp.cloudflare.com",
                        "www.gstatic.com",
                        "connectivitycheck.gstatic.com",
                        "api.ip.sb",
                    )
                ).distinct()
                val pinnedIps = runCatching {
                    // Ping path only: resolve every hostname once, in
                    // parallel, via the system resolver, and always pin it -
                    // not just when a hijack is proven. See
                    // ServerAddressPinner.resolveForPing for why.
                    ServerAddressPinner.resolveForPing(hostnames)
                }.getOrDefault(emptyMap())
                val options = pingSingboxOptions(
                    socksPort = 0,
                    obfsPort = SingboxConfigBuilder.OBFS_LOCAL_PORT,
                    workDir = workDir,
                    pinnedServerIps = pinnedIps
                )
                val result = com.lumen.app.ping.BulkSocksPinger(app).measure(
                    nodes = batch.map { it.second },
                    core = com.lumen.app.ping.BulkSocksPinger.Core.SINGBOX,
                    options = options,
                    timeoutMs = 2_500
                )
                log("singbox bulk batch ${batchIndex + 1}/${urlTestBatches.size}: ${result.log}")
                if (result.latencies.isNotEmpty()) {
                    val completedPings = mutableMapOf<String, Int>()
                    result.latencies.forEach { (index, ping) ->
                        completedPings[batch[index].first] = ping
                    }
                    // One batched write instead of one per node: with up to
                    // 150 nodes settling within the same window, N separate
                    // updatePing calls meant N Room Flow emissions landing
                    // back to back while the dashboard is on screen.
                    nodeDao.updatePingsBatch(completedPings.map { (id, ping) -> Pair(id, ping as Int?) })
                    if (_settings.value.pingAutoDeleteUnreachable) {
                        val removed = removeUnhealthyNodesAfterPing(
                            completedPings = completedPings,
                            thresholdMs = _settings.value.pingAutoDeleteThresholdMs
                        )
                        if (removed > 0) log("Auto-removed $removed node(s) after urltest batch")
                    }
                    anyResult = true
                    val measuredIds = result.latencies.keys.map { batch[it].first }.toSet()
                    batch.filter { it.first !in measuredIds }
                        .mapNotNullTo(allMissed) { pair -> targets.firstOrNull { it.id == pair.first } }
                } else {
                    log("urltest: no result for batch ${batchIndex + 1} (${batch.size} node(s)); per-node fallback")
                    batch.mapNotNullTo(allMissed) { pair -> targets.firstOrNull { it.id == pair.first } }
                    anyResult = true
                }
            }

            if (allMissed.isNotEmpty()) {
                log("urltest: ${allMissed.size} node(s) unmeasured across all batches; per-node retry")
                missedDeferred = async { pingNodeListPerNode(allMissed, bulkPingGeneration) }
            }
        }

        // Wait for both parallel batches before returning. The outer
        // pingNodeListInternal still sees a fully-settled state in its
        // own finally block, and the caller can rely on every row having
        // been written.
        ovpnDeferred?.await()
        missedDeferred?.await()
        xrayDeferred?.await()

        return anyResult
    }

    /**
     * Legacy per-node path. Only runs when the urltest bulk pass produces no
     * result at all. Kept verbatim so behaviour falls back to exactly what
     * shipped before the bulk migration.
     */
    private suspend fun pingNodeListPerNode(targets: List<NodeEntity>, generation: Long) {
        val cfg = _settings.value
        val limit = cfg.pingConcurrency.coerceIn(1, adaptivePingConcurrency)
        val targetIds = targets.map { it.id }
        val pending = java.util.Collections.synchronizedSet(targetIds.toMutableSet())
        val completedPings = java.util.Collections.synchronizedMap(mutableMapOf<String, Int>())
        val semaphore = kotlinx.coroutines.sync.Semaphore(limit)
        // coroutineScope gives this suspend fun the CoroutineScope receiver
        // `async` needs; the scope ends when every probe settles, so the
        // structured-concurrency guarantee from the previous inline version
        // (all children awaited before the caller continues) is preserved.
        val finished = kotlinx.coroutines.coroutineScope {
            val jobs = targets.map { node ->
                async {
                    try {
                        semaphore.withPermit {
                            // Real tunnel probe: spawn sing-box with this
                            // node's config, wait for the endpoint to come
                            // up, then measure an HTTP round trip through
                            // the tunnel. A node that answers a bare TCP
                            // connect but whose handshake fails must NOT
                            // read a latency here — the number on the row
                            // has to reflect whether the tunnel actually
                            // works, which is what the user sees in the
                            // Smart pick and what V2rayNG shows.
                            val ping = performRealPing(node)
                            val persistedPing = ping.coerceAtLeast(0)
                            nodeDao.updatePing(node.id, persistedPing)
                            completedPings[node.id] = persistedPing
                        }
                    } catch (e: kotlinx.coroutines.CancellationException) {
                        throw e
                    } catch (e: Exception) {
                        runCatching {
                            nodeDao.updatePing(node.id, 0)
                            completedPings[node.id] = 0
                        }
                        log("Ping failed for ${node.name}: ${e.message}")
                    } finally {
                        synchronized(pending) {
                            pending.remove(node.id)
                            if (bulkPingGeneration == generation) {
                                _pingingNodeIds.value = pending.toSet()
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            withTimeoutOrNull(PingBudget.batchMs(targets.size, limit)) {
                jobs.awaitAll()
            } != null
        }
        if (!finished) {
            val leftovers = synchronized(pending) { pending.toList() }
            runCatching { nodeDao.updatePingsBatch(leftovers.map { Pair(it, 0 as Int?) }) }
            log("Ping deadline reached: ${leftovers.size} node(s) marked unreachable")
        }
        if (finished && cfg.pingAutoDeleteUnreachable) {
            val removed = removeUnhealthyNodesAfterPing(
                completedPings = completedPings.toMap(),
                thresholdMs = cfg.pingAutoDeleteThresholdMs
            )
            if (removed > 0) log("Auto-removed $removed node(s) after the ping test")
        }
    }

    fun pingNode(node: NodeUiModel) {
        if (node.id in _pingingNodeIds.value) return
        viewModelScope.launch(Dispatchers.IO) {
            _testingNodeId.value = node.id
            // update{} instead of read-modify-write: parallel pings share these sets.
            _pingingNodeIds.update { it + node.id }
            // Old value is dropped before measuring so the row never shows a stale ping.
            _serverTestResults.update { it - node.id }
            try {
                nodeDao.updatePing(node.id, null)
                // Same repeat/aggregate rules as the bulk check.
                val entity = nodeEntities.value.firstOrNull { it.id == node.id }
                // Real tunneled ping, not TCP: the "Check" button exists to
                // answer "does this node work right now", and a bare TCP
                // handshake cannot tell a working Reality server from one whose
                // handshake is rejected. Matches what the pipeline does.
                val ping = entity?.let { performRealPing(it) } ?: -1
                val persistedPing = ping.coerceAtLeast(0)
                nodeDao.updatePing(node.id, persistedPing)
                val result = if (ping > 0) "$ping ms" else "0"
                _serverTestResults.update { it + (node.id to result) }
                log("Ping ${node.name}: $result")
                if (
                    _settings.value.pingAutoDeleteUnreachable &&
                    isPingRemovalCandidate(persistedPing, _settings.value.pingAutoDeleteThresholdMs)
                ) {
                    val removed = removeUnhealthyNodesAfterPing(
                        completedPings = mapOf(node.id to persistedPing),
                        thresholdMs = _settings.value.pingAutoDeleteThresholdMs
                    )
                    if (removed > 0) log("Auto-removed ${node.name} after the ping test")
                }
            } finally {
                // A thrown or cancelled measurement must not leave the row spinning.
                _pingingNodeIds.update { it - node.id }
                if (_testingNodeId.value == node.id) _testingNodeId.value = null
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * "Check" button on the dashboards: measures the server that is selected/connected
     * right now with the ping method chosen in settings and reports it as a Toast.
     * The value is kept in [_connectedPing] so it can also be shown next to the button.
     */
    fun checkConnectedPing(unreachableLabel: String, noServerLabel: String) {
        val node = activeNode.value
        if (node == null) {
            emitToast(noServerLabel)
            return
        }
        if (_checkingConnectedPing.value) return
        val generation = ++connectedPingGeneration
        connectedPingJob = viewModelScope.launch(Dispatchers.IO) {
            _checkingConnectedPing.value = true
            _connectedPing.value = null
            try {
                val entity = nodeEntities.value.firstOrNull { it.id == node.id }
                // Same path as pipeline: live HTTP through sing-box, no stale cache.
                val ping = entity?.let {
                    realPingCache.remove(it.id)
                    performRealPing(it)
                } ?: -1
                if (entity != null) {
                    if (ping > 0) {
                        storeRealPing(entity.id, ping)
                        nodeDao.updatePing(entity.id, ping)
                    } else {
                        nodeDao.updatePing(entity.id, null)
                    }
                }
                val label = if (ping > 0) "$ping ms" else unreachableLabel
                if (generation != connectedPingGeneration || _selectedNodeId.value != node.id) {
                    return@launch
                }
                _connectedPing.value = label
                _serverTestResults.update { it + (node.id to label) }
                emitToast("${node.name}: $label")
                log("Check ping ${node.name}: $label")
            } finally {
                if (generation == connectedPingGeneration) {

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
FILE: MainViewModel.kt (onAppForeground 4700-4870)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    private fun shouldResolveWireGuardCountry(entity: NodeEntity): Boolean {
        val protocol = entity.protocol.trim().lowercase(Locale.US)
        if (protocol !in setOf("awg", "amneziawg", "wireguard", "wg")) return false
        if (CountryFlagHelper.detectCountryStrict(entity.name, entity.server).isNotEmpty()) return false
        return !isWarpNode(entity)
    }

    private fun isWarpNode(entity: NodeEntity): Boolean {
        val outbound = entity.outboundJson.orEmpty()
        return runCatching { JSONObject(outbound).optBoolean("warp", false) }.getOrDefault(false) ||
            entity.name.contains("warp", true) ||
            outbound.contains("cloudflare", true) ||
            outbound.contains("2606:4700:110:", true)
    }

    private fun normalizedEndpointHost(server: String): String =
        server.trim().removePrefix("[").removeSuffix("]").substringBefore('%').lowercase(Locale.US)

    private fun normalizedEndpointKey(server: String, port: Int): String =
        "${normalizedEndpointHost(server)}:$port"

    private fun resolveCountryCode(host: String): String {
        val address = runCatching { InetAddress.getByName(host) }.getOrNull() ?: return ""
        if (
            address.isAnyLocalAddress ||
            address.isLoopbackAddress ||
            address.isLinkLocalAddress ||
            address.isSiteLocalAddress ||
            address.isMulticastAddress
        ) return ""
        val queryAddress = address.hostAddress?.substringBefore('%').orEmpty()
        if (queryAddress.isEmpty()) return ""
        return runCatching {
            val connection = (URL("https://ipwho.is/$queryAddress").openConnection() as HttpURLConnection).apply {
                requestMethod = "GET"
                connectTimeout = 3_000
                readTimeout = 3_000
                setRequestProperty("Accept", "application/json")
                setRequestProperty("User-Agent", "Lumen-Android")
            }
            try {
                if (connection.responseCode !in 200..299) return@runCatching ""
                val json = connection.inputStream.bufferedReader().use { JSONObject(it.readText()) }
                if (!json.optBoolean("success", true)) return@runCatching ""
                json.optString("country_code")
                    .trim()
                    .uppercase(Locale.US)
                    .takeIf { it.length == 2 && it.all { ch -> ch in 'A'..'Z' } }
                    .orEmpty()
            } finally {
                connection.disconnect()
            }
        }.getOrDefault("")
    }

    private fun stripFlagEmoji(value: String): String {
        val result = StringBuilder()
        value.codePoints().forEach { codePoint ->
            if (codePoint !in 0x1F1E6..0x1F1FF && codePoint != 0xFE0F) {
                result.appendCodePoint(codePoint)
            }
        }
        return result.toString()
            .replace(Regex("^[\\s|•·:—–-]+|[\\s|•·:—–-]+$"), "")
            .replace(Regex("\\s{2,}"), " ")
            .trim()
    }

    // ==================== Auto Mode ====================
    // Everything the app does by itself, with zero setup: a hidden built-in
    // subscription so a fresh install already has servers, a cheap protected
    // ping pass so they are sorted by real latency, and a "smart" default
    // connection target for as long as the user has never picked one by hand.

    /** Creates the built-in hidden subscription once; a no-op afterwards. */
    private suspend fun ensureAutoSubscription() {
        if (subscriptionDao.getSubscriptionSnapshot(AUTO_SUBSCRIPTION_ID) != null) return
        subscriptionDao.insertSubscription(
            SubscriptionEntity(
                id = AUTO_SUBSCRIPTION_ID,
                name = AUTO_SUBSCRIPTION_NAME,
                url = AUTO_SUBSCRIPTION_URL,
                lastUpdated = 0L,
                autoUpdateEnabled = true,
                hideUrl = true
            )
        )
    }

    /**
     * Called once per app open (see MainActivity.onStart). Ensures the hidden
     * subscription exists, then refreshes it through the exact same
     * fetch/parse/reconcile path as any user subscription
     * ([refreshSubscriptionInternal]): every supported protocol is
     * auto-detected, Base64 is decoded, a bad line is skipped rather than
     * failing the whole import, and the existing servers are left untouched if
     * the response comes back empty or broken. Once it settles, the fresh
     * server list is auto-pinged and sorted (see [autoPingOnAppForeground]).
     */
    fun onAppForeground() {
        viewModelScope.launch(Dispatchers.IO) {
            // One-time migration: users who still have the old alphabetical
            // default saved get moved to ping order, because the pipeline now
            // fills every latency and cheapest-first is what a VPN user reads
            // first. Any other explicit choice is respected.
            if (prefs.getString("servers_last_sort", null) == "DEFAULT") {
                prefs.edit().remove("servers_last_sort").apply()
            }

            realPingCache.clear()
            smartRanking.clear()

            // Cold start: wipe every stored latency so the first paint of the
            // dashboard shows "—" until fresh measurements land. Warm
            // foreground keeps the previous values visible, because the user
            // was mid-session and a stale number is better than a blank row.
            // processColdStart is set once and only reset by process death.
            val isColdStart = processColdStart
            processColdStart = false
            if (isColdStart) {
                log("onAppForeground: cold start — clearing stale pings")
                runCatching { nodeDao.clearAllPings() }
                    .onFailure { log("onAppForeground: clearAllPings failed: ${it.message}") }
                lastAutoPingCompletedAt = 0L
                lastAutoPingIds = emptySet()
            }

            // Capture the ids Room has right now. A returning install
            // already has rows, so the bulk pass can start measuring them
            // while the network round trip that replaces the list is
            // still in flight. The pass started here is a normal
            // pingNodeListInternal; if the fetch that follows changes the
            // id set, the branch further down cancels it and starts a
            // fresh pass on the new list.
            val startIds = nodeEntities.value.map { it.id }.toSet()
            // Skip the auto-pass if a ping run finished less than 5 minutes
            // ago on the same node set. Every foreground used to start a new
            // pass, so the user flipping between apps fired the whole
            // sequence repeatedly, and Cloudflare's edge saw the same burst
            // of dials as a scan and started resetting the fronted nodes.
            // Manual Ping All always runs regardless of this timestamp.
            // Start the immediate pass only on a cold start. A warm
            // foreground with the same node set already has fresh numbers
            // in Room; re-triggering caused the duplicate-pass storm the
            // id-set branch below had to cancel mid-flight. The id-set
            // branch is the only retrigger when the fetch actually changed
            // the list, and it runs on both cold and warm foregrounds.
            if (startIds.isNotEmpty() && isColdStart) {
                log("onAppForeground: cold start with ${startIds.size} cached node(s), starting immediate ping")
                pingAllCapped()
            } else if (startIds.isNotEmpty()) {
                log("onAppForeground: warm foreground with ${startIds.size} cached node(s), keeping current pings")
            }

            // Refresh the subscription in a parallel coroutine: the fetch
            // is the slow part of a cold start, and gating the ping path on
            // it meant the first measurement only began after the user had
            // already landed on the dashboard. Running it in parallel lets
            // a returning install start measuring the rows it already has,
            // while the fresh-install branch below waits for the first
            // emission before pinging.
            val fetchJob = launch {
                ensureAutoSubscription()
                subscriptionDao.getSubscriptionSnapshot(AUTO_SUBSCRIPTION_ID)?.let { sub ->
                    refreshSubscriptionInternal(sub, automatic = true)
                }
            }
            // Three independent processes below. None of them can cancel or
            // block another:
            //
            //   1. The subscription refresh keeps writing servers to Room.

package com.lumen.app.ping

import android.content.Context
import com.lumen.core.config.builder.XrayConfigBuilder
import com.lumen.core.config.parser.ParsedNode
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.coroutines.async
import kotlinx.coroutines.awaitAll
import kotlinx.coroutines.coroutineScope
import kotlinx.coroutines.sync.Semaphore
import kotlinx.coroutines.sync.withPermit
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext
import kotlinx.coroutines.withTimeoutOrNull

/**
 * Measures VLESS nodes on the in-process Xray core exposed by
 * AndroidLibXrayLite (libv2ray.Libv2ray).
 *
 * The previous process-based version spawned one Xray binary per node,
 * which paid a full runtime start plus a SOCKS loopback hop on every
 * probe and is the reason its latency ran 3-5x higher than the number
 * V2rayNG reports for the same server. This version calls
 * Libv2ray.measureOutboundDelay directly: the core runs in the app
 * process, the target URL is dialed from inside it, and the reported
 * millisecond count is the actual round trip through the proxy.
 *
 * The per-node worker is serialized inside AndroidLibXrayLite, so the
 * Semaphore only limits how many coroutines sit in the queue; it is kept
 * to bound memory and to keep behaviour identical to the previous shape.
 */
class XrayPing(private val context: Context) {

    data class PingResult(
        val latencies: Map<Int, Int>,
        val log: String,
    )

    @Suppress("unused")
    // AndroidLibXrayLite's MeasureOutboundDelay uses a process-wide
    // log handler (RegisterHandlerCreator) shared by every instance.
    // Twelve parallel measures close each other's stdout pipe and
    // roughly 30% of healthy nodes fail with "read/write on closed
    // pipe". Four matches V2rayNG's default and drops the failure
    // rate to single digits without slowing the batch: two waves of
    // nine nodes at ~500 ms per node lands in ~1 s.
    private val concurrency = Semaphore(2)

    suspend fun measure(
        nodes: List<ParsedNode>,
        timeoutMs: Int = 2_500,
    ): PingResult = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
        com.lumen.core.vpn.VpnLogBus.info(
            "XRAY_CALLED",
            "measure() entered nodes.size=${nodes.size}"
        )
        if (nodes.isEmpty()) return@withContext PingResult(emptyMap(), "empty node list")

        com.lumen.core.vpn.VpnLogBus.info(
            "XRAY_START",
            "nodes=${nodes.size} jniAvailable=${XrayBridge.isAvailable()} " +
                "xrayVersion=${XrayBridge.version()} names=${nodes.joinToString(",") { it.name }}"
        )

        if (!XrayBridge.isAvailable()) {
            return@withContext PingResult(
                emptyMap(),
                "XrayBridge unavailable (gomobile AAR missing from this build)"
            )
        }
        XrayBridge.attachFilesDir(context.applicationContext.filesDir.absolutePath)

        val latencies = withTimeoutOrNull(BATCH_TIMEOUT_MS) {
            coroutineScope {
                nodes.indices.map { index ->
                    async(Dispatchers.IO) {
                        concurrency.withPermit {
                            index to pingOne(nodes[index], timeoutMs)
                        }
                    }
                }.awaitAll()
            }
        }?.toMap()?.filterValues { it > 0 } ?: emptyMap()

        val measured = latencies.keys.toList().sorted()
        val missing = nodes.indices.filter { it !in latencies.keys }
        com.lumen.core.vpn.VpnLogBus.info(
            "XRAY_SUMMARY",
            "measured=${latencies.size}/${nodes.size} ok=$measured fail=$missing"
        )
        PingResult(
            latencies,
            "xray(jni) measured ${latencies.size}/${nodes.size}"
        )
    }

    private suspend fun pingOne(node: ParsedNode, timeoutMs: Int): Int {
        // Per-node trace path. The previous version wrote every probe to
        // one shared "xray-measure.err.log", so a parallel batch mixed
        // four nodes' debug output into the same file and the XRAY_FAIL
        // line for one node carried another node's dial log. The config
        // and the reader now agree on a name that belongs to this node
        // only.
        val tracePath = java.io.File(
            context.applicationContext.filesDir,
            "xray-measure-${node.name.hashCode()}.err.log"
        ).absolutePath

        val configJson = try {
            XrayConfigBuilder.buildMeasureConfig(
                node,
                context.applicationContext.filesDir.absolutePath,
                tracePath
            )
        } catch (e: Throwable) {
            com.lumen.core.vpn.VpnLogBus.info(
                "XRAY_FAIL",
                "node=${node.name} reason=config-${e.javaClass.simpleName} msg=${e.message}"
            )
            return -1
        }
        // TEMP: dump the config we hand to the JNI so a parsing or field
        // mismatch is visible in-app without adb.
        com.lumen.core.vpn.VpnLogBus.info(
            "XRAY_CFG",
            "node=${node.name} cfg=${configJson.replace("\n", " ")}"
        )
        // Wipe the trace file for this node only.
        runCatching {
            java.io.File(tracePath).writeText("")
        }
        // AAR internal budget is 12s x 2 attempts = up to 24s per dead
        // node; a batch of 18 nodes with 6 concurrent workers serializes
        // behind those and the UI settles in 20+ seconds. Bound the
        // coroutine at timeoutMs+500 so a dead node frees its Semaphore
        // slot early; the JNI thread keeps running to completion in the
        // background, but no consumer waits on it.
        val result = withTimeoutOrNull(timeoutMs.toLong() + 500L) {
            XrayBridge.measureOutboundDelay(configJson, TEST_URL)
        } ?: run {
            com.lumen.core.vpn.VpnLogBus.info(
                "XRAY_FAIL",
                "node=${node.name} reason=kotlin-timeout-${timeoutMs + 500}ms"
            )
            -1
        }
        if (result <= 0) {
            // Xray writes its own debug trace to MEASURE_LOG_PATH when the
            // measure config asks for it. Reading the tail of that file is
            // the only way to see the reason a probe failed without a
            // logcat connection.
            val trace = runCatching {
                val f = java.io.File(tracePath)
                if (f.isFile && f.length() > 0L) {
                    val text: String = f.readText()
                    text.takeLast(4000).replace("\n", " | ")
                } else "(no trace)"
            }.getOrDefault("(read failed)")
            com.lumen.core.vpn.VpnLogBus.info(
                "XRAY_FAIL",
                "node=${node.name} result=$result trace=$trace"
            )
        }
        return result
    }

    companion object {
        // HTTP, not HTTPS. TLS to a public target adds 3-4 round trips
        // through the tunnel that the ping pass does not need.
        // Use a raw IP so Xray's internal dialer does not need its DNS app
        // (which AndroidLibXrayLite strips out of the measure config). A
        // domain here costs an extra DNS lookup inside a resolver-less
        // core: the first attempt times out, the second succeeds from
        // cache, and the probe takes ~6s instead of ~2.5s.
        // 1.1.1.1 answers 301 -> https://one.one.one.one/generate_204 -> 404
        private const val TEST_URL = "http://cp.cloudflare.com/generate_204"
        private const val BATCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 45_000L
    }
}
